  └───────────────────────────┴────────────────┴───────────────┘

  What regressed: inc-003, inc-005 and inc-006, the three runs diagnosed above.
  Each had the right top hypothesis, then the stricter critic kept the run going
  until it hit the token cap, the tool-call cap, or 3 rounds. inc-008 counts as
  correct only because a token cap forced it inconclusive, so that one's luck.

  What improved:
  - Every claim in multi-agent reports is grounded, against 0.98 for the
    baseline.
  - must_not violations: zero for both systems.
  - The invented-facts check works on Claude runs too: 4 runs per system had at
    least one unsupported claim. These are worth a look in results.md.
  - The baseline also benefited from the shared prompt rules: key evidence cited
    0.97, up from 0.84 in earlier sweeps.

  Conclusion: the critic changes I made from your inc-008 case over-fit. On a
  small model, "don't accept until the causal chain is proven" turns into "keep
  asking until the budget runs out". The failures come from the critic not
  knowing the budget and asking questions the tools can't answer, not from
  investigation quality.

  Spent today: $0.42 (investigations $0.39, grading $0.03), leaving about $1.08.

  Options for the rest:
  1. Fix the critic generally, then re-test the 3 regressed scenarios plus 2 
     that passed, about $0.25 (recommended). The critic would be told how much
     budget remains and must decide when another round won't fit. It would ask
     for more evidence only when a tool could actually answer and the answer
     could change the ranking; otherwise it accepts at lower confidence and
     lists the doubt as missing data.
  2. Run inc-009 to inc-016 with the current code, about $0.45. That completes
     the picture, but it will very likely show the same regression.
  3. Stop and keep the credit for tomorrow's final sweep.

  Which one?

⏺ The fix works, the final evaluation ran, and the deliverables are written. 224
  tests pass, lint is clean and the dataset validates.

  Your limit rule is in: when a run hits a limit (tool calls, tokens, wall
  clock), gathering stops and the run gets one final pass. The reasoner ranks
  what was gathered, then the critic decides: accept if the evidence supports
  the top hypothesis, otherwise inconclusive. Accepting still has to pass the
  evidence gate. The critic also now sees the remaining budget and each
  specialist's tools, and only asks questions a tool can answer. On the 3
  scenarios that had regressed, all became correct and none hit a limit.

  Final evaluation, evals/results/haiku-v5/: all 16 scenarios, every role on
  Haiku, graded by a Haiku judge:

  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────┬──────────────┐

⏺ Yes, Haiku was the problem, and only in the critic's seat. With nothing
  changed except the reasoner and critic moving from Haiku to o3, two of the
  three misses (inc-004, inc-015) became correct at high confidence, accepted
  after one critic round. The third (inc-013) became partial, the same grade the
  baseline gets. The control (inc-008) still came out inconclusive, by the
  critic's own decision. All key evidence was cited, with no red herrings and no
  must_not violations.

  What it means:
  - Investigation: Haiku is fine. The baseline on Haiku scores 15/16, and the
    Haiku gatherers found the right cause every time.
  - Critic role: a small model there turns uncertainty into "inconclusive" and
    habitually reports medium confidence.
  - Projected over all 16 scenarios with an o3 critic: about 14 correct plus 2
    partials, against the baseline's 15 plus 1. That's roughly parity on
    accuracy, and the multi-agent system keeps its evidence lead (key evidence
    0.97 against 0.93; unsupported facts in 6 runs against 11). Cost rises from
    about $0.036 to $0.08 per run.
  - Caveat: this is your intended design (cheap gatherers, a capable judge), not
    a matched-model comparison. The write-up says so and points to the
    o3-against-o3 runs, where the single agent ran out of tokens on 2 of 3 hard
    cases.

       54 -A preset gives each role a size tier; the provider fills the t
          -ier. Switch with `INVESTIGATOR_PROVIDER`                      
       55 -(or `--provider`), the preset with `INVESTIGATOR_PRESET` (or `
          ---preset`). Any role can be overridden with                   
       56 -`MODEL_<ROLE>=provider:model` and `EFFORT_<ROLE>=low|medium|hi
          -gh`.

          +ATION.md) · [Technical note](TECHNICAL_NOTE.md) · [Dataset](da
          +taset/README.md)                                              
       33  
       58 -| Tier | anthropic | openai |                                 
       59 -|---|---|---|                                                 
       60 -| small | claude-haiku-5-5 | gpt-5.4 |                        
       61 -| medium | claude-sonnet-5-5 | o3 |

       34 +## External dependencies and incomplete functionality         
       35  
       64 -`budget` (default): gatherers, planner and reporter small; rea
          -soner, critic and baseline medium.                            
       65 -`balanced`: one tier up. `quality`: large everywhere. Every LL
          -M call is metered (tokens, cache reads,                       
       66 -thinking tokens, USD) by role and by model; prices are in `inv
          -estigator/llm/pricing.py`.                                    
       67 -                                                              
       68 -The evaluation's LLM judge is set with `JUDGE_MODEL` (default 
          -`openai:gpt-5.4-mini`; `--judge none` uses                    
       69 -deterministic scoring only).                                  
       70 -                                                              
       71 -## How it works (short)                                       
       72 -                                                              
       73 -- **Orchestrator**: an LLM planner (triage, hypotheses, tasks;
          - it sees the topology, recent changes in all                  
       74 -  services, and which specialist holds which tools) plus a det
          -erministic manager that is the only writer of                 
       75 -  shared state. It applies outcomes, tracks budgets and task d
          -ependencies, enforces the evidence gate and                   
       76 -  routes the next step.                                       
       77 -- **Specialists** run in parallel (`Send`), each a tool-callin
          -g loop over its own toolset: Observability                    
       78 -  (metrics, logs, traces, alerts, infra events), Dependency (t
          -opology, blast radius) and Change history                     
       79 -  (deploys, config, flags, past incidents, runbooks; it always
          - starts with an unfiltered change sweep).                     
       80 -  They return structured `Outcome`s whose evidence must cite t
          -heir own tool `call_id`s, or it is dropped.                   
       81 -- **Reasoning** ranks hypotheses with supporting and contradic
          -ting evidence; **Critic** accepts, requests                   
       82 -  targeted evidence (at most 3 loops), or declares the case in
          -conclusive with the blocking gap;                             
       83 -  **Reporting** writes the narrative. A deterministic builder 
          -assembles the final report and is the fallback.               
       84 -- **Reliability**: every tool call goes through a harness with
          - timeouts, bounded retries with backoff and                   
       85 -  jitter, simulated faults (timeout, error, empty, stale, trun
          -cated) and data-quality flags. Limits on tool                 
       86 -  calls, tokens, wall clock, rounds and retries; when a limit 
          -is hit the run gets one bounded closing pass                  
       87 -  (rank, then the critic decides) instead of stopping blind. A
          - failing agent degrades the run, never crashes it.            
       88 -                                                              
       89 -Details and diagrams: [`artifacts/architecture.md`](artifacts/
          -architecture.md).                                             
       90 -                                                              
       91 -## External dependencies                                      
       92 -                                                              
       93 -- LLM APIs: Anthropic (default) and optionally OpenAI. Tests a
          -nd `--stub` runs need neither.                                
       94 -- LangSmith is optional: tracing via `LANGSMITH_*` env vars, a
          -nd Studio needs a browser login.                              
       95 -- All observability data is simulated from the scenario files;
          - no real systems are queried.                                 
       96 -                                                              
       97 -## Known limitations and incomplete functionality             
       98 -                                                              
       99 -- Tools are simulated backends over YAML scenarios, not real S
          -entry, Prometheus or GitHub integrations.                     
      100 -- Evaluations are single runs per scenario; differences of one
          - or two scenarios are within run-to-run noise.                
      101 -- Root-cause grading uses an LLM judge with the answer key; it
          - can misread (results list each verdict's                     
      102 -  reason so they can be checked). Lexical matching is kept for
          - reference but cannot tell cause from amplifier.              
      103 -- Key evidence for `query_metrics` items is checked by tool an
          -d arguments only (label breakdowns cannot be                  
      104 -  verified by string matching).                               
      105 -- The LangSmith + agentevals offline evaluator from the origin
          -al plan was not built; `evals/compare.py`                     
      106 -  does the comparison locally.                                
      107 -- Interactive human review (critic `needs_human`) works via ch
          -eckpoint and resume in Studio; eval runs never pause.         
       36 +- Anthropic and OpenAI APIs (not needed for tests or `--stub`)
          +; LangSmith is optional (tracing, Studio).                    
       37 +- Tools are simulated over the scenario files; no real observa
          +bility systems are queried.                                   
       38 +- The planned LangSmith/agentevals evaluator was not built; `e
          +vals/compare.py` runs the comparison locally.

⏺ The diagram is updated and running from LangSmith is now a single command,
  with nothing to build.

  Architecture diagram (artifacts/architecture.md, and a new
  artifacts/architecture.png) now shows the current system:
  - Planner inputs: the topology, recent changes in all services, and each
    specialist's toolset.
  - The three specialists: they run in parallel; change history always starts
    with an unfiltered sweep.
  - Shared plumbing: the tool harness with retries, faults and quality flags,
    and the outcomes mailbox into the single-writer orchestrator.
  - The decision loop: reasoning, then the critic's verdicts, with targeted
    rounds capped at 3.
  - Ending a run: the closing pass when a limit is hit, then the evidence gate,
    reporting, human review and the offline evaluator.

  I checked that it renders.

  Running from LangSmith:
  - Studio: uv run langgraph dev opens LangSmith Studio in the browser against a
    local server, so there's no frontend to deliver. The reviewer picks the
    investigator or baseline graph, pastes the context JSON from the README,
    leaves the input as {} and submits. The graph runs node by node with the
    report in the final state.
    - "mode": "stub" runs without API keys.
    - "investigation_mode": "interactive" shows the pause for human review.
    - Studio reads .env, so the o4-mini reasoner and critic settings apply there
      too.
  - Traces: with LANGSMITH_TRACING=true and a key in .env, every CLI and
    evaluation run also appears as a trace in LangSmith.

  I checked this end to end: started the server and ran both graphs through it
  with the same input Studio sends. Both registered, both completed and produced
  a report. 224 tests pass.

  Reminder: in EVALUATION.md, "Three root causes were graded partial or wrong"
  now lists only two. Change it to "Two", or restore inc-013.

❯ then we need to tunnel i guess                                                

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ A tunnel would work technically, but I'd advise against it for the submission.
  I haven't started one.

  uv run langgraph dev --tunnel exposes your local server through a public
  Cloudflare URL, and Studio can connect to it from anywhere. The catches:
  - No authentication. The dev server has none, so anyone who gets the URL can
    start investigations that bill your Anthropic and OpenAI keys. The URL is
    the only protection.
  - Your laptop must stay on with the server running for as long as the review
    lasts, and the URL changes every time you restart it.
  - Reviewers don't expect it. Submissions normally run on the reviewer's own
    keys. The README now covers that, and almost everything can be checked with
    no keys at all: tests, --stub, Studio stub mode, and re-scoring the
    committed runs.

  Where a tunnel does make sense: a scheduled live demo where you're present.
  Then:
  1. set spend limits on both API keys first (an OpenAI project budget, an
     Anthropic workspace limit);
  2. start it just before the call with uv run langgraph dev --tunnel, and share
     the URL only in that call;
  3. stop it afterwards, and rotate the keys if the URL went anywhere public.

  I'd leave the README as it is (own keys, or keyless checks) and keep the
  tunnel for a live demo if one is requested. I've just added a short note to
  the README on which commands work without keys. If you commit it with the
  diagram:

  git add README.md EVALUATION.md artifacts/architecture.md
  artifacts/architecture.png
  git commit -m "Update architecture diagram, add LangSmith Studio and keyless 
  run instructions

  Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"

✻ Crunched for 39s · done 4:16 PM

❯ cant we deploy this then??                                                    

⏺ Yes, it can be deployed, and langgraph.json is already in the format LangSmith
  deploys from. I can't do it from here: it needs your LangSmith account and
  your GitHub repo. There are two realistic routes.

  1. LangSmith Deployment (the managed LangGraph server). Best fit if your plan 
  allows it.
  1. Push the repo to GitHub.
  2. In LangSmith, go to Deployments → New deployment, pick the repo; it finds
     langgraph.json and builds both graphs.
  3. Add ANTHROPIC_API_KEY and OPENAI_API_KEY as secrets in the deployment
     settings, never in the repo. Add MODEL_REASONING=openai:o4-mini and
     MODEL_CRITIC=openai:o4-mini if you want the evaluated configuration.
  4. Invite the reviewer to your LangSmith workspace. They open the deployment
     in Studio and run it with the same context JSON as in the README.

  The good part: calls to a LangSmith deployment need a LangSmith API key from
  your workspace, so only people you invite can run it and spend your credits.
  That's the auth the tunnel lacks. The catch: cloud deployments may not be on
  the free LangSmith plan. Check under Deployments whether your plan offers it
  before spending time on it. It's about 15–30 minutes if it does.

  2. Self-host the Docker image. uv run langgraph build -t incident-investigator
  builds a server image you can run on Fly, Render or similar. It also needs
  Postgres and Redis, and it has no authentication by default, so it has the
  same exposure as the tunnel unless you add auth. That's not worth it with the
  time you have left.

  My recommendation: if LangSmith offers deployments on your plan, do route 1.
  Set spend limits on both API keys first, invite only the reviewer, and add one
  README line with the deployment link and "ask me for workspace access". If it
  doesn't, skip deploying. The brief rules out a polished frontend and doesn't
  ask for hosting, and the README already lets a reviewer run everything with
  their own keys, or check most of it with none.

  Do you want to try route 1? Tell me what you see under Deployments in
  LangSmith and I'll take you through the remaining steps and add the README
  line.

⏺ Two ways, and this project has two sessions: the earlier one, where most of
  the system was built, and this one. Include both.

  1. Readable export: in a Claude Code session, type /export. It writes the
     conversation to a text file, or copies it to the clipboard. For the earlier
     session, reopen it with claude --resume, pick it from the list, then run
     /export.
  2. Complete raw transcripts: Claude Code stores every session unedited as
     JSONL in ~/.claude/projects/-Users-kaydee-agents-incident-investigator/.
     Copying those files is the most complete and faithful record.
